### Language

Main article: [Languages of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Bangladesh)



The [Shaheed Minar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaheed_Minar,_Dhaka), a national monument in Dhaka established to commemorate the martyrs of the 1952 [Bengali language movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_language_movement), is a symbol of Bengali nationalism.

The official and predominant language of Bangladesh is [Bengali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_language), which is spoken by more than 99% of the population as their [native language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_language).[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-LOG-271] Bengali is described as a [dialect continuum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dialect_continuum) where there are various [dialects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_dialects) spoken throughout the country. There is a [diglossia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diglossia) in which much of the population can understand or speak in Standard Colloquial Bengali, and in their regional dialect or language variety.[^cite_note-272] These include [Chittagonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chittagonian_language) which is spoken in the southeastern region of Chittagong,[^cite_note-273] [Noakhali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noakhali_language) spoken in the southern district of [Noakhali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noakhali_District)[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275] and [Sylheti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylheti_language) spoken in the northeastern region of [Sylhet](577594.md).[^cite_note-LOG-271]

English plays an important role in Bangladesh's judicial and educational affairs, due to the country's history as part of the British Empire. It is widely spoken and commonly understood, and is taught as a compulsory subject in all [schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_schools_in_Bangladesh), [colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_in_Bangladesh) and [universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_in_Bangladesh), while the English-medium educational system is widely attended.[^cite_note-276]

Tribal languages, although increasingly endangered, include the [Chakma language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chakma_language), another native Eastern Indo-Aryan language, spoken by the Chakma people.[^cite_note-277] Others are [Garo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garo_language), [Meitei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meitei_language), [Kokborok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokborok) and [Rakhine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rakhine_language). Among the [Austroasiatic languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austroasiatic_languages), the most spoken is the [Santali language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santali_language), native to the [Santal people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santal_people).[^cite_note-278] The [stranded Pakistanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stranded_Pakistanis_in_Bangladesh) and some sections of the [Old Dhakaites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhakaiyas) often use [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) as their native tongue. Still, the usage of the latter remains highly reproached.[^cite_note-279]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Bangladesh)



[Baitul Mukarram National Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baitul_Mukarram_National_Mosque), the largest and national mosque of Bangladesh

[Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Bangladesh) is the [state religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_religion) of Bangladesh.[^cite_note-state_religion-280] However, the [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Bangladesh) also upholds secularism and ensures equal rights for all religions.[^cite_note-secularism-281][^cite_note-state_religion-280] Every citizen has the freedom to practice any religion.

Islam is the largest religion in the country, followed by about 91% of the population.[^cite_note-census-6] The vast majority of Bangladeshi citizens are [Bengali Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_Muslims), adhering to [Sunni Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam).[^cite_note-state-282] The country is the third-most populous Muslim-majority state in the world and has the fourth-largest overall Muslim population.[^cite_note-283]

[Bengali Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_Hindus) form the country's [second-largest religious minority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Bangladesh) and the [third-largest Hindu community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindus#Demographics) in the world. According to the [2022 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Bangladeshi_census) Hindus form 7.95% of the total population.[^cite_note-state-282][^cite_note-census-6] In the [2011 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Bangladeshi_census), Hindus formed 8.54% of the population.

[Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Bangladesh) is the third-most followed religion, adhered to by 0.6% of the population.[^cite_note-census-6] Bangladeshi Buddhists are concentrated among the tribal ethnic groups in the [Chittagong Hill Tracts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chittagong_Hill_Tracts) and by the [Bengali Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_Buddhists) minority across coastal Chittagong, who mostly follow the [Theravada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theravada) school.[^cite_note-state-282]

[Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Bangladesh) is the fourth-largest religion at 0.3%, followed mainly by a small [Bengali Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_Christians) minority.[^cite_note-census-6] 0.1% of the population practices other religions such as [Animism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animism) or is [irreligious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion).[^cite_note-state-282]

### Education

Main article: [Education in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Bangladesh)



The [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy) of the districts of Bangladesh



[Curzon Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curzon_Hall) of the [University of Dhaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Dhaka)

The constitution states that all children shall receive free and compulsory education.[^cite_note-284] Education in Bangladesh is overseen by the [Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_\(Bangladesh\)). The [Ministry of Primary and Mass Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Primary_and_Mass_Education) is responsible for implementing policy for primary education and state-funded schools at a local level. Primary and secondary education is [compulsory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_education), and is financed by the state and free of charge in public schools. Bangladesh has a [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy_rate) of 76% as of 2021: 79% for males and 71.9% for females.[^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] Its educational system is three-tiered and heavily subsidised, with the government operating many schools at the primary, secondary and higher secondary levels and subsidising many private schools. However, government expenditure in education remains among the lowest in the world, at only 1.8% of the total GDP.[^cite_note-288]

The education system is divided into five levels: primary (first to fifth grade), junior secondary (sixth to eighth grade), secondary (ninth and tenth grade), higher secondary (11th and 12th grade), and tertiary which is university level.[^cite_note-CompEd2-289] Primary level students have to pass the [Primary Education Completion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_Education_Completion) (PEC) exam to proceed to junior secondary. The junior secondary students then give the [Junior School Certificate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_School_Certificate) (JSC) exam to get enrolled in ninth grade, while tenth-grade students have to pass the [Secondary School Certificate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_School_Certificate_\(Bangladesh\)) (SSC) exam to proceed to eleventh grade. Lastly, students have to pass the [Higher Secondary Certificate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_Secondary_Certificate) (HSC) exam at grade twelve to apply for higher education or universities.[^cite_note-290]

[Universities](2847303.md) are three general types: public (government-owned and funded by the [University Grants Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_Grants_Commission_\(Bangladesh\))), private (privately owned universities) and international (operated and funded by international organisations). The country has 55 public,[^cite_note-291] 115 private[^cite_note-292] and 2 international universities.[^cite_note-293] [National University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_University,_Bangladesh) is the [second-largest university in the world by enrolment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_universities_and_university_networks_by_enrollment).[^cite_note-294] The [University of Dhaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Dhaka), established in 1921, is the oldest public university.[^cite_note-295] [BUET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_University_of_Engineering_and_Technology) is the premiere university for [engineering education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engineering_education). The [University of Chittagong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chittagong), established in 1966, has the largest campus.[^cite_note-296] [Dhaka College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhaka_College), established in 1841, is among the oldest educational institutes in the Indian subcontinent.[^cite_note-297] Medical education is provided by 39 government, 6 armed force and 68 private [medical colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_medical_colleges_in_Bangladesh). All medical colleges are affiliated with the [Ministry of Health and Family Welfare](40314029.md).[^cite_note-298]

### Health

Main article: [Health in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Bangladesh)



Historical development of [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) in Bangladesh, displaying significant strides since independence



Historical development of [child mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_mortality) in Bangladesh since 1990, showing a significant decrease



A community health worker conducting a survey in a slum of the capital city, Dhaka

Bangladesh, by the constitution, guarantees healthcare services as a fundamental right to all of its citizens.[^cite_note-299] The [Ministry of Health and Family Welfare](40314029.md) is the largest institutional healthcare provider in Bangladesh,[^cite_note-overview-300] and contains two divisions: [Health Service Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_Service_Division) and [Medical Education And Family Welfare Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_Education_And_Family_Welfare_Division).[^cite_note-301] However, healthcare facilities in Bangladesh are considered less than adequate, although they have improved as the economy has grown and poverty levels have decreased significantly.[^cite_note-overview-300] Bangladesh faces a severe health workforce crisis, as formally trained providers make up a small percentage of the total health workforce.[^cite_note-302] Significant deficiencies in the treatment practices of village doctors persist, with widespread harmful and inappropriate drug prescribing.[^cite_note-303]

Bangladesh's poor healthcare system suffers from severe underfunding from the government.[^cite_note-overview-300] As of 2021, some 2.36% of total GDP was attributed to healthcare,[^cite_note-304] and domestic general government spending on healthcare was 16.88% of the total budget,[^cite_note-305] while out-of-pocket expenditures made up the vast majority of the total budget, totalling roughly 73%.[^cite_note-306] Domestic private health expenditure was about 75.48% of the total healthcare expenditure.[^cite_note-307] There were only 5.3 doctors per 10,000 people, and about six physicians[^cite_note-308] and six nurses per 1,000 people,[^cite_note-309] while the number of hospital beds is 9 per 1,000.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311] The specialist surgical workforce was only 3 per 100,000 people,[^cite_note-312] and there were about 5 community health workers per 1,000 people.[^cite_note-313]

Roughly 60% of the population had access to [drinking water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drinking_water) in 2022.[^cite_note-314] In 2002, it was estimated that half of the drinking water was polluted with [arsenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arsenic), exceeding levels of 10 micrograms per litre.[^cite_note-315] Bangladesh is crippled with one of the worst air qualities in the world, mostly concentrated in the densely populated urban areas, especially the capital [Dhaka](56656.md) and its [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Dhaka).[^cite_note-316] The World Bank estimated that roughly 80,000–90,000 deaths occurred in Bangladesh due to the drastic effects of air pollution in 2019. It was second-leading cause of death and disability, costing the country roughly 4–4.4% of its total GDP.[^cite_note-317]

As of 2022, the overall life expectancy in Bangladesh at birth was 74 years (72 years for males and 76 years for females).[^cite_note-318] It has a comparably high [infant mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality) rate (24 per 1,000 live births) and [child mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_mortality) rate (29 per 1,000 live births).[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320] As of 2020, [maternal mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maternal_death) remains high, clocking at 123 per 100,000 live births.[^cite_note-321] Bangladesh is a key source market for [medical tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_tourism) for various countries, mainly [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_tourism_in_India),[^cite_note-322] due to its citizens dissatisfaction and distrust over their own healthcare system.[^cite_note-323]

The main causes of death are [coronary artery disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronary_artery_disease), stroke, and chronic [respiratory disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_disease); comprising 62% and 60% of all adult male and female deaths, respectively.[^cite_note-324] [Malnutrition](258979.md) is a major and persistent problem in Bangladesh, mainly affecting the rural regions, more than half of the population suffers from it. Severe acute malnutrition affects 450,000 children, while nearly 2 million children have moderate acute malnutrition. For children under the age of five, 52% are affected by [anemia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anemia), 41% are [stunted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stunted_growth), 16% are [wasted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wasting), and 36% are [underweight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underweight). A quarter of women are underweight and around 15% have short stature, while over half also suffer from anemia.[^cite_note-325] Bangladesh was ranked 84th out of the 127 countries listed in the 2024 [Global Hunger Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Hunger_Index).[^cite_note-326]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Bangladesh)

Further information: [Culture of Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Bengal), [Museums in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museums_in_Bangladesh), and [List of libraries in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_libraries_in_Bangladesh)

### Holidays and festivals

Main articles: [Public holidays in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Bangladesh) and [List of festivals in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_festivals_in_Bangladesh)

Traditional festivals include [Pahela Baishakh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pahela_Baishakh) (Bengali New Year), which is the major festival of Bengali culture, with widespread festivities.[^cite_note-327] [Pohela Falgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pohela_Falgun) coincides with [Valentine's Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentine's_Day_in_Bangladesh), and is celebrated with a display of music, dance and other cultural activities.[^cite_note-328] Other festivals include [Nabonno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nabanna) and Poush Parbon, which celebrate new harvests of crops.[^cite_note-329] [Shakrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakrain) is an annual celebration, observed by flying [kites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kite), occurring at the end of [Poush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poush), the ninth month of the [Bengali calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_calendar). The festival coincides with [Makar Sankranti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makar_Sankranti) celebrated in India and Nepal.[^cite_note-330]



Victory Day celebration at [National Martyrs' Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Martyrs'_Memorial)

Among religious festivals, the two biggest festivals of the Muslim majority are [Eid al-Fitr](154944.md), which marks the end of the month of [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan)—and [Eid al-Adha](358226.md), which is the festival of [sacrifice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacrifice#Islam).[^cite_note-331] Both Eids are associated with the longest periods of [national holidays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Bangladesh).[^cite_note-332] Other Muslim festivals include [Mawlid](1285650.md) (Eid-e-Milad Un Nabi),[^cite_note-333] [Ashura](488563.md) on the tenth day of [Muharram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muharram),[^cite_note-334] [Chaand Raat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaand_Raat),[^cite_note-335] and [Shab-e-Barat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shab-e-Barat), [Shab-e-Meraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shab-e-Meraj), [Shab-e-Qadr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night_of_Power) during Ramadan and [Bishwa Ijtema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishwa_Ijtema).[^cite_note-336] The most celebrated Hindu festival is [Durga Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durga_Puja).[^cite_note-337] Other major Hindu festivals include [Krishna Janmashtami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna_Janmashtami), [Kali Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kali_Puja), [Saraswati Puja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saraswati_Puja), and [Ratha Yatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratha_Yatra).[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339] The biggest festival of the Buddhists across the country is [Buddha Purnima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vesak), which marks the birth of [Gautama Buddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gautama_Buddha).[^cite_note-340] Among Christians, Christmas, known as [Boro Din](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boro_Din), and [Easter](9325.md) are the most widely celebrated.[^cite_note-341]

Patriotic national festivals include the [Language Movement Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_Movement_Day), which is celebrated on 21 February in remembrance of the martyrs of the 1952 [Bengali language movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_language_movement).[^cite_note-342] It was declared as [International Mother Language Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Mother_Language_Day) by [UNESCO](21786641.md) in 1999.[^cite_note-343] [Independence Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(Bangladesh\)) is celebrated on 26 March to commemorate the [proclamation of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_of_Bangladeshi_Independence) from Pakistan.[^cite_note-star2-344] [Victory Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Day_\(Bangladesh\)) is celebrated on 16 December to celebrate the victory in the [Bangladesh Liberation War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Liberation_War).[^cite_note-star2-344] Public gatherings are observed at the [Shaheed Minar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaheed_Minar,_Dhaka) and [National Martyrs' Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Martyrs'_Memorial) during the three latter festivals to pay homage to the fallen martyrs.[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346]

### Literature

Main article: [Bengali literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_literature)



[Kazi Nazrul Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazi_Nazrul_Islam) in Chittagong, c. 1926, the [national poet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_poet) of Bangladesh.

[^cite_note-347]



[Humayun Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humayun_Ahmed) (left), a popular author and filmmaker

Bengali literature forms an important part of Bengali culture. The [Charyapada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charyapada) poems dating back to the 10th to 12th centuries are the oldest extant examples of the Bengali language.[^cite_note-348]<sup>: 40 </sup> During the [Bengal Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_Sultanate), medieval Bengali writers were influenced by Arabic and [Persian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_literature).[^cite_note-349] Milestones of the medieval age include the [Mangal-Kāvyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangal-K%C4%81vya).[^cite_note-run-350] The [Vaishnava Padavali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaishnava_Padavali) movement was led by writers such as [Vidyapati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidyapati), [Chandidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chandidas), [Govindadas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Govindadas) and [Balarama Dasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balarama_Dasa). *[Shreekrishna Kirtana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shreekrishna_Kirtana)* written by Chandidas marked a particular height of poetic achievement since the Charyapadas.[^cite_note-run-350] Other important works include [Krittibas Ojha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krittibas_Ojha)'s translation of the *[Ramayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramayana)*, [Kashiram Das](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashiram_Das)' translation of the *[Mahabharata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabharata)*, and [Maladhar Basu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maladhar_Basu)'s translation of the *[Bhagavata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhagavata)*.[^cite_note-run-350] Writers such as [Bipradas Pipilai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bipradas_Pipilai),[^cite_note-351]<sup>: 349 </sup> [Vijay Gupta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijay_Gupta_\(poet\)),[^cite_note-352]<sup>: 104 </sup> [Shah Muhammad Sagir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Muhammad_Sagir), [Zainuddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zainuddin_\(poet\)) and [Abdul Hakim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Hakim_\(poet\)) were important figures.[^cite_note-run-350] [Alaol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaol), considered a [bard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bard), is a prolific poet of medieval period.[^cite_note-run-350]

The [Bengal Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_Renaissance) from the late 18th century to the early 20th century had a profound effect on modern Bengali literature. [Michael Madhusudan Dutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Madhusudan_Dutt) invented the [blank verse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blank_verse) in Bengali literature.[^cite_note-353] [Mir Mosharraf Hossain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mir_Mosharraf_Hossain) was the first prominent Bengali Muslim writer.[^cite_note-354] [Lalon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lalon), a [fakir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fakir) practising [Sufism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufism) and [sādhanā](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%81dhan%C4%81) influenced the [bauls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baul).[^cite_note-355] [Sarat Chandra Chattopadhyay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarat_Chandra_Chattopadhyay) wrote about the characteristics of the Bengali society.[^cite_note-356]

[Rabindranath Tagore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabindranath_Tagore) was the first Asian and non-European laureate of the [Nobel Prize in Literature](23385442.md).[^cite_note-357] [Kazi Nazrul Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazi_Nazrul_Islam) was a revolutionary poet who espoused political rebellion against colonialism and fascism.[^cite_note-358] [Jibanananda Das](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jibanananda_Das) was the most recognised Bengali poet after Tagore and Nazrul.[^cite_note-359] [Begum Rokeya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Begum_Rokeya) is regarded as the pioneer feminist writer of Bangladesh.[^cite_note-360] [Syed Mujtaba Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syed_Mujtaba_Ali) is noted for his [cosmopolitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmopolitanism) views.[^cite_note-361] [Jasimuddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasimuddin) was a renowned pastoral poet, popularly called *Palli Kabi* (folk poet).[^cite_note-362] [Farrukh Ahmad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farrukh_Ahmad) is considered the poet of the "Islamic Renaissance".[^cite_note-363] [Syed Waliullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syed_Waliullah) was a notable novelist.[^cite_note-pied-364]

[Shamsur Rahman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamsur_Rahman_\(poet\)) and [Al Mahmud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Mahmud) are considered two of the greatest Bangladeshi poets to have emerged in the late 20th century. [Ahmed Sofa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Sofa) is regarded as the most important intellectual in the post-independence era.[^cite_note-365] [Sufia Kamal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufia_Kamal) was a major feminist writer.[^cite_note-366] [Humayun Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humayun_Ahmed) was the most popular author in post-Independence Bangladesh.[^cite_note-pied-364] [Shahidul Zahir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahidul_Zahir) was widely acclaimed for his usage of [magical realism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magical_realism).[^cite_note-367] Other major writers include [Akhteruzzaman Elias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akhteruzzaman_Elias), [Shawkat Osman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shawkat_Osman) and [Syed Shamsul Haq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syed_Shamsul_Haq).[^cite_note-pied-364] [Selina Hossain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selina_Hossain) is a prolific female author in the modern era.[^cite_note-368] [Muhammad Zafar Iqbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Zafar_Iqbal) is a pioneer science fiction writer.[^cite_note-369] [Anisul Hoque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anisul_Hoque) is a popular contemporary literary figure.[^cite_note-370] The annual [Ekushey Book Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekushey_Book_Fair) and [Dhaka Lit Fest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhaka_Lit_Fest), organised by the [Bangla Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangla_Academy), are among the largest literary festivals in South Asia.[^cite_note-371]

### Architecture

Main article: [Architecture of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Bangladesh)

Further information: [Architecture of Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Bengal) and [List of archaeological sites in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_archaeological_sites_in_Bangladesh)



[Somapura Mahavihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somapura_Mahavihara) in [Naogaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naogaon), a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Bangladesh)



The [Sixty Dome Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixty_Dome_Mosque) in [Bagerhat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagerhat), a UNESCO World Heritage Site



The [Ahsan Manzil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahsan_Manzil) in [Old Dhaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Dhaka)

The architecture of Bangladesh is intertwined with that of the [Bengal region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Bengal) and the broader [Indian subcontinent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_India). It is influenced by the country's [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Bangladesh), [religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Bangladesh) and [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Bangladesh).[^cite_note-372] Hindu and Buddhist architectural remnants have been found in [Mahasthangarh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahasthangarh), which dates back to the 3rd century BCE.[^cite_note-re-373]<sup>: 157 </sup> [Nandipada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nandipada) and [Swastika](45943.md) symbols have been found on stone querns in the [Wari-Bateshwar ruins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wari-Bateshwar_ruins), which indicate the presence of [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) in the area during the [Iron Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age)—from 400 to 100 BCE.[^cite_note-374] The [Somapura Mahavihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somapura_Mahavihara) built under the rule of the Buddhist [Pala Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pala_Empire) in the 8th century is a notable example of the pre-Islamic era.[^cite_note-375] Other Buddhist [vihāras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vih%C4%81ra) include [Shalban Bihar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalban_Bihar) in [Mainamati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainamati) and [Bikrampur Vihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bikrampur_Vihara) in [Bikrampur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bikrampur).[^cite_note-re-373]<sup>: 164–167 </sup> Recent excavations have also uncovered new evidence of pre-Islamic smaller temples which served the Hindu, Buddhist and [Jain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism) populations of the area.[^cite_note-sat-376] [Indo-Islamic architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Islamic_architecture) can be seen from the 13th century, especially in the distinctive mosque architecture of the [Bengal Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_Sultanate), an example being the [Sixty Dome Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixty_Dome_Mosque) among others in the [Mosque City of Bagerhat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosque_City_of_Bagerhat)—which is a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Bangladesh).[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-sat-376]

[Mughal Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Bengal) saw the spread of [Mughal architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_architecture) in the region. Examples in Dhaka include the [Bara Katra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bara_Katra) and [Choto Katra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choto_Katra) in Old Dhaka, the [Sat Gambuj Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sat_Gambuj_Mosque) in [Mohammadpur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammadpur_Thana) and the [Musa Khan Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musa_Khan_Mosque) in [Curzon Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curzon_Hall).[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] Notable Mughal-era forts include the [Lalbagh Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lalbagh_Fort) in [Old Dhaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Dhaka), the [Idrakpur Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idrakpur_Fort) in [Munshiganj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munshiganj)—and the [Hajiganj Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hajiganj_Fort) and the [Sonakanda Fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonakanda_Fort) in [Narayanganj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narayanganj), respectively.[^cite_note-380] The [Kantajew Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kantajew_Temple) and [Dhakeshwari Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhakeshwari_Temple) are examples of late medieval [Hindu temple architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_temple_architecture).[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] Bengali [vernacular architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vernacular_architecture) is noted for pioneering the [bungalow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bungalow).[^cite_note-383] [Panam Nagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panam_Nagar) in [Sonargaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonargaon) exhibits architectural influence from the Sultanate, Mughal, British and hybrid colonial traditions.[^cite_note-384]<sup>: 22 </sup> [Indo-Saracenic architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Saracenic_architecture) flourished during the [British Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj),[^cite_note-385] examples include the [Curzon Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curzon_Hall) of the [University of Dhaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Dhaka), the [Chittagong Court Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chittagong_Court_Building), [Rangpur Town Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rangpur_Town_Hall) and [Rajshahi College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajshahi_College). The [zamindar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zamindar) gentry built many palaces in the latter style, including the [Ahsan Manzil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahsan_Manzil), the [Baliati Zamnidar Bari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baliati_Zamindari), the [Tajhat Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tajhat_Palace), the [Rose Garden Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Garden_Palace), the [Dighapatia Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uttara_Gonobhaban), the [Puthia Rajbari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puthia_Rajbari), [Natore Rajbari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natore_Rajbari) and the [Mohera Zamindar Bari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohera_Zamindar_Bari). [Muzharul Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muzharul_Islam) is considered to be a pioneer of [modernist movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_architecture) in Bangladesh and South Asia.[^cite_note-386] [Louis Kahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Kahn) is a notable foreign architect who designed the [National Parliament Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jatiya_Sangsad_Bhaban) in [Sher-e-Bangla Nagar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sher-e-Bangla_Nagar).[^cite_note-387]

### Visual arts, crafts and clothing

Main articles: [Bangladeshi art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladeshi_art), [Sculpture of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sculpture_of_Bangladesh), and [Textile industry in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textile_industry_in_Bangladesh)

Further information: [Muslin trade in Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslin_trade_in_Bengal)



Embroidery on [Nakshi kantha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakshi_kantha) (embroidered [quilt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quilt)), a centuries-old Bengali art tradition



*[Aparajeyo Bangla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aparajeyo_Bangla)*, a sculpture dedicated to the [Bangladesh Liberation War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Liberation_War), located on the campus of the University of Dhaka

The recorded history of art in Bangladesh can be traced to the 3rd century BCE, when [terracotta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terracotta) sculptures were made in the region. In classical antiquity, notable sculptural [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_art), [Jain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jain_art), and [Buddhist art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_art) developed in the [Pala Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pala_Empire) and the [Sena dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sena_dynasty). The [Bengal Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_Sultanate) saw [Islamic art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_art) evolve since the 14th century. During the Mughal rule, [Jamdani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamdani), a fine-muslin textile, was woven with Persian [motifs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motif_\(textile_arts\)) in Dhaka.[^cite_note-388]<sup>: 239 </sup> It was classified by UNESCO as an [Intangible cultural heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intangible_cultural_heritage) in 2013.[^cite_note-389] Bangladesh also produces the [Rajshahi silk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajshahi_silk), a fine silk noted for its softness and use in patterned textiles.[^cite_note-390]<sup>: 158 </sup> [Ivory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivory), [brass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brassware_industry_in_Bangladesh) and [pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pottery_of_Bangladesh) have deep roots in Bangladeshi culture.[^cite_note-391]<sup>: 483–485 </sup> The [Nakshi Kantha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakshi_Kantha), a centuries-old [embroidery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embroidery) tradition for [quilts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quilt) in Bengal, is made throughout Bangladesh.[^cite_note-392]<sup>: 44 </sup> The [modern art](163543.md) movement in Bangladesh took shape in post-independence [East Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Bengal), especially with the pioneering works of [Zainul Abedin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zainul_Abedin).[^cite_note-star-393] Other leading painters include [SM Sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SM_Sultan), [Mohammad Kibria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Kibria), [Safiuddin Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safiuddin_Ahmed), [Shahabuddin Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahabuddin_Ahmed_\(artist\)), [Kanak Chanpa Chakma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanak_Chanpa_Chakma),[^cite_note-394] [Qayyum Chowdhury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qayyum_Chowdhury), [Rashid Choudhury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashid_Choudhury), [Quamrul Hassan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quamrul_Hassan), [Rafiqun Nabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafiqun_Nabi) and [Syed Jahangir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syed_Jahangir).[^cite_note-star-393]

[Novera Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novera_Ahmed) is the pioneer of modernist sculpture in Bangladesh.[^cite_note-395] Other eminent sculptors include [Nitun Kundu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitun_Kundu), [Syed Abdullah Khalid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syed_Abdullah_Khalid), [Hamiduzzaman Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamiduzzaman_Khan), [Shamim Sikder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamim_Sikder), [Ferdousi Priyabhashini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdousi_Priyabhashini) and [Abdur Razzaque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdur_Razzaque_\(artist\)).[^cite_note-396] The annual [Mangal Shobhajatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangal_Shobhajatra) (Bengali New Year parade) organised by the [Faculty of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faculty_of_Fine_Arts,_University_of_Dhaka) of the University of Dhaka on [Pohela Boishakh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pohela_Boishakh) was enlisted as an Intangible cultural heritage by UNESCO in 2016.[^cite_note-397] Photography as a form of art has seen rapid growth in the 21st century. [Chobi Mela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chobi_Mela_International_Photography_Festival), held biennially, is considered the largest photography festival in Asia.[^cite_note-398]

[Lungi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lungi) is the most common informal clothing for men,[^cite_note-399] while [kurta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurta) (*panjabi*) and [pajama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pajama) are worn by men on festivals and holidays.[^cite_note-400] Domestically tailored [suits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suit_\(clothing\)), [neckties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neckties) and [pants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trouser) are customarily worn by men at formal events, and the traditional [sherwani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sherwani) and [churidar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Churidar) are worn along with the [turban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turban) in weddings.[^cite_note-arts-401] Women commonly wear the [shalwar kameez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalwar_kameez) accompanied by [orna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dupatta); while [sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sari) is worn on more formal events.[^cite_note-arts-401] Some women follow [Islamic clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_clothing).[^cite_note-402]

### Performing arts

Main articles: [Music of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Bangladesh) and [Dance in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance_in_Bangladesh)



Dance in celebration of [Pohela Falgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pohela_Falgun) at the [University of Chittagong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chittagong)



A [baul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baul) playing the [ektara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ektara) at the shrine of [Lalon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lalon) in [Kushtia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kushtia)

[Theatre in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_in_Bangladesh) includes various forms with a history dating back to the 4th century CE.[^cite_note-Ahmed-403] It includes narrative forms, song and dance forms, supra-personae forms, performances with scroll paintings, puppet theatre and processional forms.[^cite_note-Ahmed-403] The [Jatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jatra_\(theatre\)) is the most popular form of Bengali folk theatre.[^cite_note-404] Apart from the various forms of [Indian classical dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_classical_dance), including the *[Kathakali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathakali)*, *[Bharatanatyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatanatyam)*, [Odissi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odissi)[^cite_note-405] and [Manipuri dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manipuri_dance)—native dance traditions that developed across the country.[^cite_note-arts-401]

[Music of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Bangladesh) can be classed into classical, light-classical, [devotional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devotional_music), and [popular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_music). Classical music in Bangladesh is represented by the common forms of devotional music across the Indian subcontinent, such as the [Hindustani classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustani_classical_music) genre [dhrupad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhrupad) and [khayal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khayal). Other major forms include [qawwali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qawwali) and [kirtan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirtan).[^cite_note-arts-401] [Rabindra Sangeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabindra_Sangeet) and [Nazrul Sangeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazrul_geeti) retain their popularity.[^cite_note-406] Native [folk music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_music) features the [baul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baul) mystical tradition, which was popularised by [Lalon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lalon) in the 18th century, and is listed by UNESCO as a [Masterpiece of Intangible Cultural Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpiece_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity).[^cite_note-wei-407][^cite_note-alom-408] Other native lyric-based forms of folk music include [bhatiali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhatiali), [bhawaiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhawaiya), [dhamail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhamail), [kavigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavigan), [jarigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jarigan), [sari gan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sari_gan), marfati, and [gombhira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gombhira).[^cite_note-arts-401][^cite_note-alom-408]

Folk music is accompanied by instruments such as the [ektara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ektara), [dotara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dotara), [dhol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhol), [bansuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bansuri) (a type of flute), mandira, [khanjani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khanjani), [sarinda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarinda_\(instrument\)), [khamak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khamak), [dugdugi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duggi_\(drum\)), [juri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jori_\(instrument\)), jhunjhuni and [majira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taal_\(instrument\)) (a type of [cymbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cymbal)).[^cite_note-alom-408] Bangladesh has a rich tradition of [Indian classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_classical_music), which uses instruments like the [sitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sitar), tabla, [sarod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarod), and [santoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santoor).[^cite_note-409] Musical organisations and schools such as the [Bangladesh Shilpakala Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Shilpakala_Academy) and [Chhayanaut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chhayanaut) have helped preserve the traditions of Bengali folk music.[^cite_note-alom-408]

[Sabina Yasmin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabina_Yasmin) and [Runa Laila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Runa_Laila) are considered two of the greatest female playback singers in the country.[^cite_note-410] [Andrew Kishore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Kishore), another leading playback singer, is considered the "King of Playback".[^cite_note-411] [Azam Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azam_Khan_\(singer\)), nicknamed the "Pop Samrat" and the "Rock Guru", is a founding figure of [Bangladeshi rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladeshi_rock).[^cite_note-412] Musicians such as [Ayub Bachchu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayub_Bachchu) and [James](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_\(musician\)) have also gained nationwide popularity.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] [Shayan Chowdhury Arnob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shayan_Chowdhury_Arnob) has been an influential figure in [indie rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indie_rock).[^cite_note-415] Popular pop singers in the 21st century include [Habib Wahid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habib_Wahid) and [Tahsan Rahman Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahsan_Rahman_Khan).[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417] Influential [heavy metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_metal_music) include [Artcell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artcell) and [Warfaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warfaze).[^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419]

### Media and cinema

Main articles: [Media of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Bangladesh) and [Cinema of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Bangladesh)

The history of press in Bangladesh dates back to 1860, when the first printing press was established in Dhaka.[^cite_note-dr-420] Bangladesh has a diverse media landscape, including commercial and state-owned outlets.[^cite_note-bbc1-421] Prominent news agencies in Bangladesh include [Bangladesh Sangbad Sangstha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Sangbad_Sangstha) (BSS) and [Bdnews24.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bdnews24.com).[^cite_note-bbc-422] Television is the most popular form of media consumption.[^cite_note-bbc1-421] [Bangladesh Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Television) (BTV) is the sole state-owned television network with nationwide coverage.[^cite_note-423] Private television networks include [ATN Bangla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATN_Bangla), [Channel I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_I), [NTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NTV_\(Bangladeshi_TV_channel\)), [RTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTV_\(Bangladeshi_TV_channel\)), [Ekushey TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekushey_TV), [Ekattor TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekattor_TV), [Jamuna TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamuna_TV) and [Somoy TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somoy_TV).[^cite_note-bbc-422] Print media is the second-most widely consumed, and newspapers are privately owned and outspoken, including [The Daily Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Star_\(Bangladesh\)), [Dhaka Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhaka_Tribune), [The Financial Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Financial_Express_\(Bangladesh\)), [Bangladesh Pratidin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Pratidin), [Kaler Kantho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaler_Kantho), [Prothom Alo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prothom_Alo), [The Daily Ittefaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Ittefaq) and [Jugantor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jugantor).[^cite_note-bbc-422][^cite_note-bbc1-421]



Headquarters of [Ekushey Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ekushey_Television) on a high-rise in [Kawran Bazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawran_Bazar), Dhaka

[Bangladesh Betar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Betar) is the lone state-run radio service.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-bbc1-421] [Radio Foorti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Foorti), [Radio Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Today), [Radio Aamar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Aamar) and ABC Radio were popular privately owned radio stations; popularity of radio has declined significantly.[^cite_note-bbc-422][^cite_note-bbc1-421] Popular foreign media include [BBC News](1139893.md) ([BBC Bangla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Bangla)), [CNN](62028.md), [VOA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VOA) and [Al Jazeera](30204052.md).[^cite_note-bbc1-421] [Indian television drama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_television_drama) has a substantial presence on Bangladeshi satellite television.[^cite_note-425][^cite_note-bbc1-421] [Freedom of the press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_the_press_in_Bangladesh) remains a major concern due to government attempts at censorship and the harassment of journalists. Bangladesh ranked 152nd out of the 180 countries listed in the 2026 [World Press Freedom Index](8264708.md), among the lowest rankings in the world.[^cite_note-426] The [cinema of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Bangladesh) dates back to a screening of a [bioscope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movie_camera) in 1898.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 129 </sup>[^cite_note-back-428] The [Nawabs of Dhaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawab_of_Dhaka) patronised the production of several [silent films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silent_film) from the 1900s.[^cite_note-back-428] Picture House, the first permanent cinema in Dhaka, began its operation during the year between 1913 and 1914.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup> *[Sukumari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukumari_\(film\))* (The Good Girl), released in 1929, was the first film produced in Bangladesh.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup> *[The Last Kiss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Last_Kiss_\(1931_film\))*, the first full-length feature film, was released in 1931.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup>[^cite_note-back-428] By 1947, a total of 80 cinemas were listed.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup> The first Bengali-language film in East Pakistan, *[Mukh O Mukhosh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukh_O_Mukhosh)* (Face and Mask), was released in 1956.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup>[^cite_note-back-428] *Akash ar Mati* (Sky and Earth), released in 1959, was the second film as such.

The [Bangladesh Film Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Film_Development_Corporation) was founded in Dhaka as the East Pakistan Film Development Corporation in 1957–1958, as a full-service film production studio.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup>[^cite_note-back-428] [Zahir Raihan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zahir_Raihan) made various influential films throughout the period, notably *[Kokhono Asheni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokhono_Asheni)* in 1961, *Shangam* in 1964 (the first [colour film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Color_print_film) in Pakistan), and *Jibon Theke Neowa* in 1970.[^cite_note-back-428] The film industry in Dhaka positioned itself as the base for a Bengali Muslim cinema from the 1960s onward.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup> The first movie post-Independence, *[Ora Egaro Jon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ora_Egaro_Jon)*, was directed by [Chashi Nazrul Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chashi_Nazrul_Islam) and released in 1972.[^cite_note-back-428] At the industry's peak, about 80 movies were produced each year between 1996 and 2003, a number which has constantly declined thereafter.[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup>[^cite_note-back-428] Prominent directors include [Khan Ataur Rahman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khan_Ataur_Rahman), [Alamgir Kabir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alamgir_Kabir_\(film_maker\)), [Amjad Hossain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amjad_Hossain), [Humayun Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humayun_Ahmed), [Morshedul Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morshedul_Islam), [Tanvir Mokammel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanvir_Mokammel), [Tareque Masud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tareque_Masud), [Salahuddin Lavlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salahuddin_Lavlu) and [Enamul Karim Nirjhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enamul_Karim_Nirjhar).[^cite_note-easmin-427]<sup>: 130 </sup> Tareque Masud was honoured by [FIPRESCI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIPRESCI) at the 2002 [Cannes Film Festival](101460.md) for his film *[Matir Moina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matir_Moina)* (The Clay Bird). [Film societies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_society) have contributed to the development of cinema in Bangladesh.[^cite_note-429]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Bangladeshi cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladeshi_cuisine)

Further information: [Bengali cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_cuisine)



*[Kala bhuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kala_bhuna)*, a [beef dish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_beef_dishes) from [Chittagong Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chittagong_Division)



[Rohu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohu) curry, a fish-based dish



Chicken [pilaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilaf) (*Morog Polao*), a traditional dish from [Old Dhaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Dhaka)

Bangladeshi cuisine has been shaped by the nation's geographic location, climate, and history.[^cite_note-cuisine-430]<sup>: 14 </sup> The staple dish is [white rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_rice), which along with fish, forms the culinary base. Varieties of [leaf vegetables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leaf_vegetable), potatoes, [gourds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gourd) and [lentils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lentil) ([dal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dal)) also play an important role. [Curries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curry) of beef, [mutton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutton), chicken and duck are commonly consumed,[^cite_note-shaheda-431] along with multiple types of [bhortas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhurta) (mashed vegetables),[^cite_note-432] *bhajis* ([stir fried](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stir_frying) vegetables) and [tarkaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarkari) (curried vegetables).[^cite_note-cuisine-430]<sup>: 8 </sup> Mughal-influenced dishes include [kormas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korma), kalias, [biryanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biryani), [pulaos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilaf), [teharis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahri_\(dish\)) and [khichuris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khichuri).[^cite_note-shaheda-431] Among the various used spices, [turmeric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turmeric), [fenugreek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fenugreek), [nigella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigella), [coriander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coriander), [anise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anise), [cardamom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardamom) and [chili powder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chili_powder) are widely used; a famous spice mix is the [panch phoron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panch_phoron). Condiments and herbs used include [red onions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_onion), [green chillies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chili_pepper), garlic, [ginger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ginger), [cilantro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cilantro), and [mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mentha).[^cite_note-cuisine-430]<sup>: 12 </sup> [Coconut milk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut_milk), [mustard paste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustard_\(condiment\)), [mustard seeds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustard_seeds), [mustard oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustard_oil), [ghee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghee), [achars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_pickle)[^cite_note-shaheda-431] and [chutneys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chutney) are also widely used in the cuisine.[^cite_note-cuisine-430]<sup>: 13–14 </sup>

Fish is the main source of protein, owing to the country's riverine geography, and it is often eaten with its [roe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roe). The [hilsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilsa) is the national fish and is widely consumed. Other highly consumed fish include [rohu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohu), [pangas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pangasius_pangasius), and [tilapia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tilapia).[^cite_note-433] [Lobsters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobster), [shrimps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrimp) and [dried fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dried_fish) (*shutki*) also play an important role in local cuisine.[^cite_note-cuisine-430]<sup>: 8 </sup> In Chittagong, dishes involving beef include [kala bhuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kala_bhuna) and [mezban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mezban), the latter being a traditionally popular feast, featuring the serving of *mezbani gosht*, a hot and spicy beef curry.[^cite_note-cuisine-430]<sup>: 10 </sup>[^cite_note-shaheda-431][^cite_note-guardiancuisine-434] In Sylhet, the *[shatkora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shatkora)* lemons are used to marinate dishes, a notable example is [beef hatkora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beef_Hatkhora).[^cite_note-guardiancuisine-434] Among the tribal communities in the [Chittagong Hill Tracts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chittagong_Hill_Tracts), cooking with [bamboo shoots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamboo_shoot) is popular.[^cite_note-435] Khulna is known for using *chui jhal* ([piper chaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piper_chaba)) in its meat-based dishes.[^cite_note-guardiancuisine-434][^cite_note-shaheda-431] *Kalai ruti*, a type of rustic [flatbread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flatbread), is widely consumed in [Chapai Nawabganj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapai_Nawabganj) of Rajshahi.[^cite_note-436]

Bangladesh has a wide range of desserts, including [sweets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweets) such as the *[roshogolla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasgulla)*, *[roshmalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasmalai)*, *[chomchom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chomchom)*, *[sondesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandesh_\(confectionery\))*, *[mishti doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mishti_doi)* and *[kalojaam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulab_jamun)*, and *[jilapi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalebi)*.[^cite_note-437] [Pithas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitha) are traditional boiled desserts made with rice or fruits.[^cite_note-438] [Halwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halwa), [shemai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shemai) and [falooda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falooda), the latter two variations of [vermicelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vermicelli), are popular desserts during religious festivities.[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] [Ruti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roti), [naan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naan), [paratha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paratha), [luchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luchi) and [bakarkhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakarkhani) are the main local breads.[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-shaheda-431] Hot [milk tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milk_tea) is the most commonly consumed beverage in the country, being at the centre of [group conversations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adda_\(South_Asian\)).[^cite_note-443] [Borhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borhani), [mattha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattha) and [lassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lassi) are popular traditionally consumed beverages.[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445] [Kebabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kebab) are widely popular, particularly [seekh kebab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seekh_kebab), [chapli kebab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapli_kebab), [shami kebab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shami_kebab), [chicken tikka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicken_tikka) and [shashlik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shashlik), along with various types of *chaaps*.[^cite_note-shaheda-431] Popular street foods include [chotpoti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chotpoti), [jhal muri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jhal_muri), *shingara*,[^cite_note-446] [samosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samosa) and [fuchka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panipuri).[^cite_note-447]

### Sports

Main article: [Sports in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Bangladesh)



[Bangladesh cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_cricket_team)

In rural Bangladesh, several [traditional indigenous sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_traditional_games) such as [Kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi), [Boli Khela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boli_Khela), [Lathi Khela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lathi_Khela) and [Nouka Baich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nouka_Baich) remain fairly popular. While Kabaddi is the national sport,[^cite_note-448] [Cricket](25675557.md) is widely described as the most popular sport in the country. The [national cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_national_cricket_team) played their first [Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket_World_Cup) in 1999 and were granted [Test cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test_cricket) status the following year. Bangladesh reached the quarter-final of the [2015 Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Cricket_World_Cup), the semi-final of the [2017 ICC Champions Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_ICC_Champions_Trophy) and reached the final of the [Asia Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia_Cup) 3 times – in 2012, 2016, and 2018. [Shakib Al Hasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakib_Al_Hasan) is widely regarded as one of the greatest [all-rounders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-rounder) in the history of the sport.[^cite_note-449] In 2020, the [Bangladesh national under-19 cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_national_under-19_cricket_team) won the men's [Under-19 Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Under-19_Cricket_World_Cup).[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] The Bangladesh national under-19 cricket team also won the [U-19 Asia cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Under-19_Men's_Asia_Cup) in 2023 and 2024 consecutively.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453] In 2018, the [Bangladesh women's national cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_women's_national_cricket_team) won the [2018 Women's Twenty20 Asia Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Women's_Twenty20_Asia_Cup).[^cite_note-454]



[Bangladesh football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_national_football_team)

[Football](10568.md) is the second-most popular sport in Bangladesh, following cricket.[^cite_note-455] The first instance of a national football team was the emergence of the [Shadhin Bangla football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shadhin_Bangla_football_team) during the liberation war of 1971.[^cite_note-456] On 25 July 1971, the team's captain, [Zakaria Pintoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zakaria_Pintoo), became the first person to hoist the Bangladesh flag on foreign land before their match in neighbouring India.[^cite_note-457] Following independence, the [national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_national_football_team) made its debut in 1973 and eventually qualified for the [AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Asian_Cup) (1980), becoming only the second South Asian team to do so.[^cite_note-458] Notable achievements in Bangladeshi football include the [2003 SAFF Gold Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_SAFF_Gold_Cup) and [1999 South Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_1999_South_Asian_Games). The [Bangladesh women's national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_women's_national_football_team) won the [SAFF Women's Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAFF_Women's_Championship) consecutively in 2022 and 2024.[^cite_note-459] Bangladesh archers Ety Khatun and Roman Sana won all 10 gold medals in the [archery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archery) events (individual and team) in the [2019 South Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_South_Asian_Games).[^cite_note-460] The [National Sports Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Sports_Council) regulates 42 sporting federations.[^cite_note-461] [Chess](5134.md) is very popular in Bangladesh. Bangladesh has five grandmasters in chess. Among them, [Niaz Murshed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niaz_Murshed) was the first grandmaster in South Asia.[^cite_note-462] In 2010, mountain climber [Musa Ibrahim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musa_Ibrahim) became the first Bangladeshi climber to conquer [Mount Everest](42179.md).[^cite_note-dailystardetail-463] [Wasfia Nazreen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wasfia_Nazreen) is the first Bangladeshi climber to climb the [Seven Summits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Summits) and the [K2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K2).[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465] Babar Ali became the first Bangladeshi to climb [Mount Makalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Makalu) in May 2026.[^cite_note-466]

Historians believe that Bengal, the area comprising present-day Bangladesh and the Indian state of [West Bengal](34040.md), was settled in about 1000 B.C. by Dravidian-speaking peoples who were later known as the Bang. Their homeland bore various titles that reflected earlier tribal names, such as Vanga, Banga, Bangala, Bangal, and Bengal.

[^cite_note-38]: &#x20;Dyson, Tim (2018). *A population history of India: from the first modern people to the present day* (1st ed.). Oxford: Oxford University Press. pp. 80–81. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-0-19-882905-8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-0-19-882905-8). However, in assessing the likely scale of the mortality ... there were no demographic data for any significant part of Bengal. Indeed, many Company administrators seldom ventured from Calcutta. The few local reports on famine deaths were for small and unrepresentative populations. Also, as we noted in Chapter 4, cultivators could be very mobile, and they often abandoned their villages at times of famine. Much of any 'depopulation' was undoubtedly caused by out-migration. In addition, birth rates tend to fall during famines, and this too would have reduced the population ... To sum up, the 1769–70 famine was certainly exceptional in terms of Bengal's experience during the eighteenth century (although crop losses from a cyclone and a shift in a river course also contributed to many famine deaths in 1787–88). It is very unlikely, however, that the 1769–70 crisis involved the deaths of 10 million people. Indeed, on Datta's assessment, even a figure of 5 million may well lie outside the plausible range. However, famine mortality and large-scale out-migration did cause significant depopulation in large parts of Bengal—from which it evidently took several years to recover. As late as 1773, Company officials regarded the revival of the province's economy as requiring substantial return-migration from adjacent territories—including the, then, independent state of Awadh.
